# Fidelity register

Every place where the port does not, or cannot, reproduce the pinned Win16 `civ.exe` exactly, or
where the original's behaviour is unknown (prompt section 4 item 9). Each entry gives the
address, the reason, the chosen behaviour and the findings or open-question id. Entries are added
as the design and the translation reach them; the register is complete only at the phase 7 gate.

Labels: **choice** (a port decision where the original is unknown or cannot be reproduced),
**not modelled** (original state the port does not keep), **inferred** (a platform fact from
documentation or reasoning, not observed in the original). From extension unit D4b also **DOS listing
(model)**: a frame, time or picture of the DOS profile computed by the port's model of the ideal DOS
machine (F125) from the DOS listing's constants and control flow, not observed in a DOS run.

## Memory, handles and the emulated stack

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | dynamic selectors (KERNEL GlobalAlloc) | Windows 3.1's selector values and reuse order are unknown | Deterministic allocator from 0x1428 up in steps of 8 (multi-tile blocks take sel, sel+8, ...). A freed selector is quarantined while never-used selectors remain (about 3,450 allocations); after that the selector freed longest ago is reused, so a stale far pointer faults until its selector is reused and then aliases the new block. This departs from the prompt's "never reused" (5.2 item 5): every sprite draw allocates and frees a block, so selectors would run out (review finding B2, **choice**, `DESIGN.md` 3.1; **confirmed as implemented by the user, 2026-10-11 about 08:00 UTC**). Selector values and reuse order differ from Windows 3.1 | 5.2 item 5; review B2 |
| F2 | HWND, HDC, GDI objects, timer ids, HFILE, Borland fds | The original numeric values are unknown | Port schemes of `DESIGN.md` 3.6: even, non-zero, per-kind pools, never-used values first, then FIFO reuse (**choice**). The values are observable through `g_cities[-1]` (findings U1, G10, Q89) | U1, G10, Q89 |
| F3 | return-address segment words; callback frames | The original's run-time code selectors and the frames of USER/GDI/KERNEL code are unknown | Return addresses use the virtual code selectors 0x1000 + 8(n-1); calls from `w16` into `core` push `ra = 0xFFF0:kind`; the words of USER/GDI/KERNEL frames are not modelled, so a callback's frame starts directly below the words `w16` pushed (**choice / not modelled**, `DESIGN.md` 3.4) | review N2 |
| F4 | words that rt pushes from ES | rt keeps no ES register model across core code | A `push es` in rt pushes the last ES that rt loaded (**not modelled**); the emulated stack is masked in comparisons with other programs (`DESIGN.md` 3.4, 9) | port design, no finding (5.2 item 7) |
| F5 | MakeProcInstance / FreeProcInstance | Instance thunks are not observable by game code | MakeProcInstance returns the original code value, FreeProcInstance does nothing (**choice**, "instance thunk values") | port design, no finding (5.5) |
| F6 | SI/DI words at Win16 API calls | API frames are not modelled (F3), and USER/GDI/KERNEL may hold their own values in SI/DI when they call back into the game | The translator mirrors compiler scratch uses of SI/DI only where a ported callee (game or run-time) saves them; at an API call the port's SI/DI are the game caller's (**not modelled**) | 5.2 item 7 |
| F7 | PicDecompress 10B8:18E1, 190D, 19B9, 19E5, 1A7E, 1AA9 (`pushaw` ... `popaw`) | The six `pushaw`/`popaw` pairs around the huge decrements of `writePtr` write 16 bytes of register images (AX, CX, DX, BX, SP, BP, SI, DI) below SP and pop them again; AX..DX hold compiler temporaries of the inline-assembly decoder | The port does not write these 16 bytes (**not modelled**): the region lies below PicDecompress's SP and is rewritten by the next call's argument and frame words. If a rule 6.6 class (a) last-writer analysis ever names one of these words, this entry must be revisited | 5.2 item 7 |
| F8 | PicDecompress 10B8:175C `std` | The row-wrap copy sets the direction flag and never clears it, so the original can return with DF = 1 | The port has no DF: the copy is written as the backward byte loop the listing performs; the only other string instructions of live code are in the run-time, and each of them runs after a `cld` of its own routine or of its caller (`__StorePackedBCD` 1000:098D for `__StoreFourBCDDigits`, `VprinterFlushChunk` 1000:1A3D for `VprinterStrlen`/`VprinterPutHexDigit`; listing check 2026-10-08), so no later string instruction depends on the leaked DF; Windows code is not modelled (**inferred** from the listing) | listing 10B8:175C, no finding |
| F9 | huge pointer difference (`hp_sub`) | Borland subtracts the tile numbers `sel >> __AHSHIFT` with a borrow from the offsets (10B8:1776..1788) | `hp_sub` computes exactly that (the difference of the linear values modulo 2^32); for the port's selectors (multiples of 8) it equals the prompt's `((selA - selB) >> 3) << 16` form | 5.2 item 4 |
| F71 | DX, the high word of the 32-bit result, at the handled-message returns of NameDialogWndProc 10F0:0266 (02F7, 02FB, 0326), CityScreenWndProc 1030:9C17 (9C59, 9CC8), PediaDialogSubclassProc 1068:282D (28D2, 2919) and CustomizeWorldWndProc 1108:0705 (074D) | On these paths the listing sets only AX (`xor ax,ax`, `mov ax,1`, the gray brush) and leaves DX as the last callee or the caller left it (0x1420 on the mouse-button paths of CityScreenWndProc and PediaDialogSubclassProc); the recovered C returns 0 or 1 as an `LRESULT` (RECOVERY-ERRATA E18, rule 6.6 class (c)) | The port returns DX 0 (**choice**: `gen/annot.py` `ret_dx` cannot express a 32-bit result's high word). No reader of that DX exists, so no difference is observable: each procedure is reached only through MakeProcInstance/SetWindowLong (10F0:0137, 1030:0134, 1068:275C, 1108:033E), never by a direct call or CallWindowProc (the listing has none); all 15 DispatchMessage calls (1008:0F6C, 1030:9B77, 1038:0CFC, 1068:27A6, 1070:1094, 1098:0359, 0395, 05F7, 06AF, 10B8:073F, 1140:0032, 11E0:09AA, 0B32, 11E8:02E5, 1220:18B1) discard the result; every game SendMessage to these windows discards it; the only SendMessage results the game uses are list-box messages sent to list boxes, low word only (1098:1B2F, 1B5C, 1C4D, 1C62, 1CDA, 1170:1C35); IsDialogMessage returns a BOOL. **Platform fact (inferred)**: USER takes only the 16-bit HBRUSH from WM_CTLCOLOR and ignores the results of the other handled messages (WM_PAINT, WM_COMMAND, WM_DRAWITEM, the mouse and character messages, 0x401). The port's receivers agree (WM_CTLCOLOR truncated to 16 bits; WM_COMMAND and WM_DRAWITEM results discarded; DM_GETDEFID, the one 32-bit consumer, reaches the procedures' DefDlgProc path, which passes DX:AX through) | RECOVERY-ERRATA E18; findings 8.2 1030:9C59, 1108:074D; verify/seg031_10F0_r.md, seg007_1030_p3.md, seg014_1068_p2_r.md, seg034_1108_r.md (round 7 "dx"); docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-15 |

## Faults and out-of-range accesses

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F10 | 1030:298E (C2 masks 0xEF..0xFF) | The original reads past segment 12D8 with an unknown result under Windows 3.1 | The port stops with a "general protection fault" dialog that names the active statement and the data address (the C2 fault run `p49-c2-fault`: statement 1030:28A7, address 12D8:4642; `DESIGN.md` 3.1, 3.3) | findings C2 |
| F11 | rt K1 path (stray `pop si`, 1000:4922) | The stray decrement lands at an address that depends on a selector value | Reproduced on the emulated stack and logged when it runs (`rt/rt_heap.c`) | findings K1 |
| F33 | the return of any core function whose saved DS, saved BP or return address was overwritten in the emulated stack (overrunning copies: MenuZ 1220:035A C10, DrawScreenTextWithShadow 10D8:036D C12, IntroMovieCallback 11E0:029C C12, DrawTextAtPen 10D8:00A2 C12, DrawTextAtPortXY 10D8:016E C12, GetScreenTextWidth 10D8:05D2 C12, TruncateStringToWidth 1098:001E C12, RunListBoxDialog 1098:1B0F and 1098:1C62 C12) | The original pops the overwritten words into DS and BP and returns through the overwritten address: a load of an invalid selector or a jump into arbitrary code, with no recoverable outcome | `fr_leave` compares the control words with those `fr_enter` pushed and, if one differs, stops with the "general protection fault" dialog naming the function's address (`DESIGN.md` 3.4, "Copies that overrun a frame object"); latent with the shipped resources | findings C10, C12; 5.3 |
| F58 | StartCivilWar 11A0:066D/0678; TransferCityToCiv 11A0:0E55, 0E64, 0E76, 0E93 | On the first pass of the distance loop `cityIndex` (bp-0x1A) is unwritten (class (b), `DESIGN.md` 3.4). After a visible conquest its last writer is SetPort's `inc bp; push bp` (SetGrPort's BP+1, via AnimateUnitMove 11C0:3012), a stack offset of a modelled game frame; with Enemy Moves off and `g_autoFlushToWindow` != 0 it is the map window's client width, which SCOPY@ (DrawMapTile 1110:0D32) copies from the rectangle USER.GetClientRect stored (class (b) by value origin); with `g_autoFlushToWindow` 0 and on hidden moves, earlier frames (not traced). A stack offset or a width of 128 or more makes `g_cities[cityIndex]` at 0678 and in TransferCityToCiv (the owner store 0E55, the y, x and size reads 0E64/0E76/0E93) address DGROUP outside the 128-entry table; a width below 128 transfers that existing city slot (possibly another civ's or a free one) to the rebels | The accesses are performed as the listing performs them, with the port's emulated-stack offset (or client width) as the index. On the visible path, under the inferred initial SP 0xD8BE (`DESIGN.md` 3.1), the index is 0xD6F7 and the record is 1420:D800..D81B, WinMain's `keyState[0x68..0x83]` (bp-0x116 = 1420:D798, filled once by USER.GetKeyboardState at 1008:02E6; the port's GetKeyboardState copies its emulated key table): the size (+7) is keyState[0x6F], x/y (+4/+5) keyState[0x6C]/[0x6D], and the owner store (+0xB) writes keyState[0x73]. Each pass of the distance loop (05F0..06B9) that finds no city farther than 1 adds that size to `rebelPop` (0 at 05E8) and transfers the same record again, and the loop runs while `rebelPop*9/(g_unknownSetting42EE+3) < g_civTotalCitySize[civ]` (0692..06B7; the victim's total is reduced only at 06BC, after the loop). With the key-state byte 0 (the usual state, and always in a headless port run) the loop never ends while the victim's total city size is > 0: a hang without message pumping, in both programs. A toggle bit (1) ends it; a down bit (0x80/0x81, cbw -128/-127) makes `rebelPop` fall until the 16-bit product wraps. The same holds for every stale index, since each pass re-adds and re-transfers the same record: on the client-width path the size byte at `g_cities[width]` decides (0: the same hang, e.g. a width below 128 selecting a slot whose size byte is 0; positive: the loop ends; negative: it falls until the product wraps) (verify/seg053_11A0_r.md round 8 N2). SetTileOwner with x/y 0..255 is bounded: GetScreenBufferPixel/SetScreenBufferPixel ignore coordinates outside the 320x200 buffer (11E8:0DDF..0DF1, 0E2A..0E3C; inside it the write changes a buffer byte, effects not traced); 0EA3 adds the size to `g_civTotalCitySize[newCiv]`. That record lies below DGROUP's limit (no fault); an address beyond the limit takes the fault policy (`DESIGN.md` 3.3). The checked build records each access (`/* OOB: findings G35 */` at the governing markers feeds gen/oob_allowlist.py). The round-6 class (a) headless test is withdrawn: the site is class (b), and a test of the visible path would hang under the port's default key state unless it sets a key state or bounds the loop | findings 8.2 11A0:066D, G35; verify/seg053_11A0_r.md round 6 R6-T1, round 7 R7-T1, R7-T2, R7-N1; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-09; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-16, F7-17; verify/seg053_11A0_r.md round 8 N1, N2 |
| F59 | MoveCivUnits 11F0:13CB | The fortress completion test reads `g_terrainTypes[terrainType].moveCost` with the stale index of F57 and no range test; an index outside 0..23 reads other bytes | The read is performed as the listing performs it (`shl bx,3`, `es:[bx+g_terrainTypes]`); the checked build records it (`/* OOB: findings G48 */`, keyed to the access 13CB, at marker 138C feeds gen/oob_allowlist.py); possible outcomes: any signed byte + 4 as the completion threshold, or the fault policy for an address beyond the segment's limit (`DESIGN.md` 3.3) | findings G48; verify/seg063_11F0_p1.md round 6 R6-T2; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-03 |
| F64 | FrameRectLogical 1220:10B6 | `g_egaToSystemColor[egaColor]` (10B9..10BE) has no range test; DrawCasualtyReport (1018:0BCF) and ShowTopFiveCities (1080:0C94) pass 16 + civ in colour mode and 0xF in monochrome mode; ShowWondersOfTheWorld (1188:0D28, 0D14..0D1A) and ShowHistorianReport (1188:11CE, 11BB..11C0) pass 16 + civ in both modes (neither reads `g_monochromeMode`: the segment's last test is 1188:0A08, in ShowPowerGraph); the fifth caller, ShowPowerGraph, passes 0. So indexes 16..23 read bytes 12D8:45CE..45D5 past the 16-entry table | The port reads the same bytes, which are `g_civLightColor[civ]` (as port P2): the box is framed in the civ's light colour. The checked build records the reads (`/* OOB: port P4 */` at marker 10B6 feeds gen/oob_allowlist.py); the recovered C (`SetForeColor(g_egaToSystemColor[egaColor])`) matches the listing, so no erratum | port P4 (scenarios S4/S5, indexes 16..23); inventory/out-of-range.md; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-11; verify/seg069_1220_p1_r.md round 7 R7-N1; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-10 |
| F65 | OpenPalaceView 1158:0F8A (reads 0F94, 0FB1, 0FC0, 0FDE); DrawPalaceSectionPiece 1158:142A, 143B, 1451, 146B, 14A3 (called at 0FE5; 146B also through the call 0E45) | After the section loop a stale `leftEndSection` (F48) indexes `g_palaceSectionVariant` / `g_palaceSectionLevel` (12 words each) without a range test at 0F94/0FB1/0FC0/0FDE and, when the variant word is 1 and the index is not 3, is passed to DrawPalaceSectionPiece (0FE5) as `section`, with `g_palaceSectionLevel[stale]` as levelForY; that function indexes `g_palaceSectionLevel[section]` (142A), `g_palaceSectionVariant[section]` (143B), `g_palaceSlotPieceTypes[section][half]` (1451), the y-offset table 1350:000A by levelForY/style/pieceType (146B) and `g_palaceSprites[section][half]` (14A3), all without a range test. At 0DCF/0E1D the guards limit the section index to 1, 2, 4, 5 / 3, 4, 6, 7 (the shape-1 case runs only for sections 2, 3, 5, 6), so no section index leaves the tables there. The 0E45 call can still pass the centre section 4 with half 1 (`rightEndSection` 4 with si = 3, a middle wing, and equal variants: a stale `rightEndSection` after section 8 was built through port P3's '`' key (F48) whose word is 4, or a loaded save with sections 5..7 unbuilt and section 4 built, which needs a save because game code always builds sections 3..5 at 10E8:0E8A..0EBA; verify/round11-R11A.md N5). The centre's half-1 piece type is never written (ComputePalaceSlotPieces writes only half 0, 1158:1397; OpenPalaceView reset it to 0xFF at 095C..0967), so pieceType is -1 and the 146B read of `g_palacePieceYOffsets[levelForY][style][-1]` returns the byte before that row (1350:0009, the high byte of `g_palacePieceWidths[4]`, when levelForY and style are 0): INDEX3, and EXTENT in that case, under marker 1445 (verify/seg044_1158.md round 9 R9-N1; verify/round10-R10C.md N2). Latent: the stale `leftEndSection` needs `g_palaceSectionLevel[1..3]` all -1, which no game code produces (F48): a loaded save or a call before setup; the 0E45 case needs a stale word of 4 (its value is not traced) or such a save | The reads are performed as the listing performs them; the checked build records them (`/* OOB: port P5 */` at marker 0F8A and at DrawPalaceSectionPiece's markers 1421, 1432, 1445 and 1472 feeds gen/oob_allowlist.py); possible outcomes: end pieces chosen and drawn from bytes past the palace arrays (another sprite handle, a stray y offset); a sprite word of 0 (also a section whose sprites were never loaded), for which DrawTileToPortPixels (11A8:15F4) shows the 'No Tile Data!' error box (ShowErrorMessage 11A8:1601) and draws nothing; a sprite word that is not a live handle (a section loaded earlier and since freed, since FreePalaceSectionSprites leaves the freed handle in place, or bytes past the array), which DrawTileToPortPixels passes to KERNEL.GlobalLock without a check (11A8:161A; the result for an invalid handle is host behaviour, not checked); or the fault policy for an address beyond the segment's limit (`DESIGN.md` 3.3). The port performs the same reads and calls | port P5; verify/seg044_1158_r.md R6-N2 and round 7 R7-N1, R7-N2, R7-N3; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-22; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-07, F7-08, F7-09; verify/seg044_1158.md round 8 N2, round 9 R9-N1; verify/round10-R10C.md N2; docs/verify/round8-fixes.md F8-20 |
| F66 | DeleteNonLandUnitCallback 11C0:22BB; DeleteLandUnitCallback 11C0:2270 | DestroyCity walks the owner's unit table from FindUnitAtTile's index, which can belong to another civ (1170:041E..0435, G67); when the owner's slot with that index is free, its type byte 0xFF gives `g_unitTypes[-1]`, the record at 1420:1A22 before the table, inside `g_improvementTypes`: `domain` (+2) is the word at 1A24, improvement 42's required advance, the constant 43, and `capacity` (+0x10) the word at 1A32, the constant 60 | The reads are performed as the listing performs them; the checked build records them (`/* OOB: findings G67 */` at 11C0:22BB and at 11C0:2270 feed gen/oob_allowlist.py; scenario S5 recorded 22BB 3 times). Outcomes: the word is 43, so DeleteUnit always runs on the free slot with type -1 (the index -1 effects of findings C3). It reads `capacity` 60 (1710) and the slot's link (172A); with a link other than 0xFF it calls GetTerrainType of the slot's stale square (1766) and, on Ocean, ForEachUnitInStack(owner, slot, DeleteLandUnitCallback) (177D), which starts with the free slot itself (2270 reads `g_unitTypes[-1].domain` = 43: not deleted) and continues along the stale link, deleting the owner's land units in that ring (up to 10 callbacks). The DestroyCity walk likewise continues from the free slot along the link ForEachUnitInStack fetched before the callback (1F10): a slot never used in the process has link 0 (`g_units` lies in the zeroed BSS and InitCivilization sets only type and status), so the owner's unit 0 and its ring lose their ships and aircraft, more than G67 states; a slot left by an earlier game or a load keeps its old link and square. RemoveUnitFromTile (1899) then unlinks a slot that is in no ring (not traced). Reachable whenever a G67 event meets a free slot whose link is not 0xFF: the DestroyCity continuation always for a never-used slot (link 0, unit 0's ring), and for slots left by an earlier game or a load. The 2270 access needs Ocean at the slot's stale square: a never-used slot's square (0,0) is Arctic or Tundra on every map the game builds (LabelBodiesAndFinalizeMap 1028:0F14..0F23 fills row 0 with colour 15, 0F3C.. sets squares of rows 0/1 to colour 7; `g_terrainByMapColor` maps 15 to Arctic and 7 to Tundra), so only a reused slot (an earlier game in the process, or the saving process, then loaded) whose stale square is Ocean reaches it, and the allowlist entry is kept for those; in-game terrain changes at (0,0) were not checked. Port model probe data (self-consistency): scenarios S2..S5 recorded no access at 2270. The round-6 inference that a free slot's link is 0xFF (docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-24) is withdrawn | findings G67, C3; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md F6-24; verify/seg057_11C0_p2_r.md round 7 R7-T1, R7-T2, round 8 N1; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-18, F7-19 |
| F72 | LabelBodiesAndFinalizeMap 1028:0BF8, 0C86 (marker 0C7F), 0CA9, 0E2D (marker 0E0F) | The label allocation loop 0C7F..0CA1 (`inc newLabel` while `g_bodySizes[kind][newLabel] != 0`) has no limit at 63 (findings C13). With a 64th live label of one kind, `g_bodySizes[kind][label]` (64 words per kind) is indexed past its row at 0BF8 and 0CA9, and the relabel read `labelRemap[label]` (bp-0xB8, 64 words) at 0E2D, with the label byte 0..255 as index, leaves the object: labels 64..79 read `rankedLabels` (bp-0x38); 80..90 the scalar locals bp-0x18..bp-0x4 (`terrain` .. `newLabel`), whose words hold the function's own last stores once each local has been stored in the call (class (a); stack-resident since the round-9 fix F10-01, verify/seg005_1020.md round 9 R9-T1); before a local's first store in the call its word is from before the call (not traced, as for labels 95 and up): `newId` (stored only at 0E30) at the first projected cell (x 0, y 0 of the kind-0 projection), `relabelY`/`relabelX` (0BB9/0BC1) before the first merge, `upLabel` (0B99) before the first square of the current kind on row 1 or later, `dx` likewise (0B7C), unless the ranking's 0D79 stored it before the projection (verify/seg005_1020.md round 10 R10-N1); 91..94 the control words (the DGROUP selector, the saved BP+1, a stack offset exact only under the inferred initial SP of `DESIGN.md` 3.1, and the return address with its virtual code selector, F3); 95 and up the caller's frame (not traced). An id read there can be 16 or more, and later readers of the body id that assume 1..15 then index past their objects too, e.g. ComputeCitySiteValues 1028:1277 (`g_bodyCitySiteCounts[id]++`, 16 words: ids 16..143 land in `g_bodySizes`; marker 125C), BalanceStartPositions' `startsPerBody`, InitCivilization; these readers are not listed: a scenario that reaches the C13 condition must triage them (5.2 item 3) | The accesses are performed as the listing performs them. Whether 64 labels of one kind can be live at once is open (findings C13; reachability low). The checked build records each access (`/* OOB: findings C13 */` at 0BF8, 0C86, 0CA9 and 0E2D feed gen/oob_allowlist.py); with at most 63 live labels every body id is 1..15 and nothing is recorded (scenarios S2..S5 recorded nothing here) | findings C13; verify/seg005_1020.md round 8 R8-T1, round 9 R9-T1, R9-N1, round 10 R10-N1; verify/seg030_10E8_p2.md round 8 R8-N1 (supersedes round 5 N3); docs/verify/round8-fixes.md |

A production byte of 99 arises when ChooseCityProduction returns 99 (the return 1170:065D) for a civ in `g_humanCivsMask` on one of two paths: the dialog path (Auto Production off, 05F7..0620) when GR_DialogOpen fails (0651..065D); or Auto Production (city status bit 0x10: 0601..0609 skip the dialog, so GR_DialogOpen is not called) when there is no domestic candidate (18A2) or the best domestic score is above 500 (18A9), 18BE → 065D. No effect on the game: RunNewGameSetup's InitCivilization sets g_advanceSource to -1 for every civ (10E8:1417..1434) after Customize World, a load replaces it (SerializeGameData 10F8:0D77), and its only readers are ShowPediaEntry's "learned from" lines after the game began. In the DOS graphics D5e's I-custom consumes Escape (F166, F171) and FCE does not name dialog 143, so the code is not reached there. The original's defect, kept (the base prompt's rule "Keep the original's bugs"; its fault policy: never corrupt host memory). Recorded by unit FCE (2026-10-17), no behaviour change. Open item for X9: confirm the write's target and its harmlessness in the final audit (STATUS "Test open items") | `ext-FCE-record.md` ("Customize World's Escape"); EXT-HANDOFF (FCE's X9 side finding); F193 |

Possible outcomes: with a value outside 0..7 every adjacent unit counts as foreign (the usual case in both programs); a value equal to a civ id excludes that civ's units, and for the human's id CheckAdjacentForeignUnits also reveals the occupant to the human | findings 8.2 1088:0E14; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md (orchestrator item 7); moved here from "Faults and out-of-range accesses" (verify/seg018_1088_p1.md round 7 R7-N2; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-20) |
| F68 | DrawMiniPalace 1088:1D00 | The pen word (bp-0x12) read for section 8 is the HPEN that SelectObject returned at 1AB9 (a handle Windows assigned; class (b) by value origin) | The port's pen handle differs (F2); the word is only compared with -1, which neither program's handle equals, so the outcome is the same: no observable difference | findings 8.2 1088:1D00; verify/seg018_1088_p2.md round 6 and round 7 R7-N1; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md (orchestrator item 7); moved here from "Faults and out-of-range accesses" (docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-20) |
| F69 | BuildSeaZoneLinks 1028:1899 (load path) | On the load path `dstBodyId` (bp-0x1A) is the source-selector argument slot of KERNEL.hmemcpy, last written by BlockMove's push of that selector (1100:024D; `g_saveBuffer`'s selector from GlobalLock 10F8:0057), a selector Windows assigned (class (b) by value origin; world generation is class (a), `DESIGN.md` 3.4). Whether KERNEL's hmemcpy rewrites its own argument slot is unknown (**not modelled**) | The port's selector differs (F3). If hmemcpy leaves the slot alone, the word is a selector, which never equals a body id (the port's dynamic selectors start at 0x1428; a Windows selector of an application's block is not below 0x100, **platform fact (inferred)**), so the comparison at 1899 fails in both programs: no observable difference. If KERNEL rewrote the slot, the original's word and the comparison's outcome are unknown | findings 8.2 1028:17B9, G62; verify/seg006_1028.md N8 and round 7 R7-N1, R7-N2; docs/verify/round6-fixes.md (orchestrator item 7); moved here from "Faults and out-of-range accesses" (docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-20, F7-22) |
| F70 | ChooseCityProduction 1170:187C (read 18B6) | For an AI city whose candidates all score >= 999, or with one candidate, `bestIndex` (bp-0xE) is read without a write (184E..1872, 187C). On the turn loop (ProcessCity 1030:273D) its last writer is the far call of PlayerKnowsAdvance at 1030:7E7D inside CivHasActiveWonder (1030:1AC8), which pushes the code selector of segment 1030 there, a selector Windows assigned (F3); with an active Hoover Dam, GetScreenBufferPixel's frames write it later (via GetBodyId 1AF9 its saved DS, then via GetBodyId 1B36 the saved BP+1, a stack offset, which remains). Class (b) by value origin; the ReselectCityProduction path (1030:2386), ProcessCity's mode-0 AI calls 1030:6A43/6C2F and the other AI callers were not traced (`DESIGN.md` 3.4) | 187C..18B6 return the word at SS:(BP - 0xE6 + 2*index) mod 0x10000 as the AI city's new production (ProcessCity 274F stores its low byte); that address lies outside `items` (73 words at bp-0xE6) for every index except 0x0000..0x0048 and 0x8000..0x8048, for which (2*index) mod 0x10000 < 0x92 wraps it back to `items[index & 0x7FFF]` (a candidate below itemCount or an unwritten slot). The port's virtual selector 0x1030 at its BP 0xD58A gives 1420:F504, beyond DGROUP's limit 0xE4BF (`DESIGN.md` 3.1), so the port takes the fault policy (`DESIGN.md` 3.3); in the original the outcome depends on the selector Windows assigned to segment 1030: a word of `items` for a selector in those two ranges (whether Windows assigns one is host behaviour, not checked), otherwise a read inside DGROUP's limit (a production value taken from an arbitrary word) or a general protection fault. On the Hoover path the index is a stack offset, which also addresses a word outside `items` (inside the segment's limit for the port's frame chain; inference, not computed per path). The checked build records the read (`/* OOB: port P6 */` at marker 187C feeds gen/oob_allowlist.py). Port model probe data (self-consistency): the soaks S4/S5 reached 187C 230 times, always after the 17FE write | findings 8.2 1170:187C; port P6; verify/seg047_1170_p2.md round 7 T1, round 8 N1, N2; docs/verify/round7-fixes.md F7-01; docs/verify/round8-fixes.md F8-16, F8-17 |

the item rectangles are 16-bit (wrong beyond about 2,047 rows from the top; the game asks for item 0 only). **RV4 P47-R2:** before 2026-10-15 the game's list dialogs (Find City 131, the Civilopedia) repainted their client snapshot over the list's scroll bars and border until a scroll redrew them. **User decision (2026-10-11 about 08:00 UTC):** the scroll bar is shown when the dialog opens if and only if the list holds more items than fit on one page, so that the player sees there are more pages. **Fixed (RV4 P47-R2, 2026-10-15; `p47r2-record.md`, review `verify/p47r2-review.md`, verdict accept):** two **choices** implementing the user's decision. (1) A frame that the paint of a parent without WS_CLIPCHILDREN covered is marked at the parent's EndPaint and drawn again by the window's next BeginPaint (WM_NCPAINT, wParam 1, before its update region and WM_ERASEBKGND), so the game's list dialogs show the list's border and, when the items need more than one page, its scroll bar once the opening paints are done, and again after each Civilopedia hide/show. The mark alone is no reason for WM_PAINT: a parent paint that covers only a child's frame and not its client area leaves that frame covered until the child paints for another reason (not reachable in the game, whose list dialogs invalidate and repaint the whole list with the dialog; review finding 7). (2) A standard bar shown or hidden by SetScrollRange repaints the window itself only (its frame at once, its client area and children invalidated with the synchronous erase, WM_SIZE when the client size changes) and exposes nothing in its parent or below. Before, it exposed the window's rectangle as a MoveWindow to the same rectangle does: the dialog got WM_NCPAINT and the synchronous WM_ERASEBKGND (with DefDlgProc's WM_CTLCOLOR to the dialog procedure), and every other top-level window meeting the list's rectangle (the map window, and the Status and World Map windows where they overlap) got WM_NCPAINT, WM_ERASEBKGND and a pending WM_PAINT; in the many-cities Find City case the map window's WM_PAINT then stalled RunListBoxDialog's loop (1098:1BEE) for ever (seen in the harness; by code reading the same would have hung civ64, F86). Rule 2 removes those messages in that case only (no base run reached it). Messages added to game window procedures: none (only w16's LISTBOX procedure gets the new WM_NCPAINT). **Inferred:** Windows 3.1 sets its per-window "frame needs painting" state when the non-client area is invalidated and serves it by a show's synchronous paint or the next BeginPaint, not after a parent's later paint, so it most likely showed the same artifact; marking at the parent's EndPaint is the port's own rule, and whether Windows 3.1 showed the snapshot over the bar remains not known. For a bar shown or hidden, Windows 3.1 redraws the changed frame only (inferred). Tests: `test_user` (`test_p47r2`), `p4tests.py` `findcity` and `listbar`. User's visual check pending (`manual-checklist-ext.md` P47-1). **Before P47b (pre-existing, review finding 1, MAJOR):** while a list dialog was open, any exposure that gave a window other than the dialog an update region (for example the game screen following a host resize, F110, while Find City or the Civilopedia list was open; `expose` ignores occlusion) made RunListBoxDialog's loop (1098:1BEE: PeekMessage, IsDialogMessage only, no dispatch) retrieve that window's WM_PAINT for ever with the virtual clock standing still; in civ64 no host input was admitted meanwhile (F86), so the game hung. **Fixed (P47b, 2026-10-15; `p47b-record.md`):** a loop that passes another window's WM_PAINT on without dispatching it (RunListBoxDialog 1098:1BEE) still gets it again and again, as in Windows 3.1, but a PeekMessage that returns the same WM_PAINT again with no update region validated and no time charged since is an idle poll (1 ms, a wait that takes host input; `queue.c` "An undispatched WM_PAINT"; **choice**); the dialog answers keys and closes, and the window is painted after it closes. **Inferred:** Windows 3.1 spins there too while real time passes, and input, retrieved before WM_PAINT, still reaches the dialog. Exposure still ignores occlusion (**choice**, `user_wnd.c` header "Exposure ignores occlusion"): clipping would not remove the hang and would change many base runs' paints. No base output changed. Tests: `test_queue` (`test_paint_again`), `test_user` (`test_p47b`), `test_host_win32` (the host path), `p4tests.py` `listbar` (`p49-findcity-bar` stops at its virtual-time limit; `p49-findcity-resize`: the host view grows while Find City is open, the dialog closes, the last frame is 1024x768). User's check pending (`manual-checklist-ext.md` P47-2) | DESIGN.md 5; `p47r2-record.md`; `verify/p47r2-review.md`; `p47b-record.md` |

## Host (Win32 back end, phase 5)

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F81 | keyboard characters (`platform/win32/host_win32.c` key_down, `platform/w16/host_sync.c`) | Windows 3.1 translated keys to characters of the installed code page with its keyboard driver (ToAscii); the host translates to UTF-16 with its own layout | The host's TranslateMessage gives the character; its Windows-1252 byte (WC_NO_BEST_FIT_CHARS) is attached to the key down. A character with no Windows-1252 byte is dropped (counted, `hw_dropped_chars`); the key down itself is still posted. Dead keys are composed by the host; Alt+keypad character codes are not composed (the host's character arrives at the Alt release, which carries none). Keys still down when the host window loses the focus are released; with Alt down a Tab press comes first, as on Windows 3.1's Alt+Tab (**choice**; prompt 5.5 "Input"; review P5MT-2, P5MT-3) | prompt 5.5; F32 |
| F82 | closing the host window (its close box or its system menu's Close; `host_sync.c`) | The original ran inside the Windows 3.1 desktop, where the program's own windows had the close commands | WM_SYSCOMMAND SC_CLOSE is posted to the active emulated window, as Alt+F4 on that window would, and the game's own close handling decides (the map window's WM_CLOSE reaches the Quit command, 1208:05E1, F78). With no active window nothing happens (**choice**). Alt+F4 pressed in the host window is not the host's: it reaches the game as keys, and the emulated window manager turns it into the same SC_CLOSE (F78). **Superseded by UX1 for the host window (2026-10-15, the user's request of about 14:03 UTC):** neither the host's close (the close button, the Exit item, WM_CLOSE from outside) nor Alt+F4 posts SC_CLOSE to the game any more; they are the host's exit, at once before the game window opens and with the warning in the game (F160). A WM_QUIT in the host pump still becomes HOST_EV_QUIT, which posts this SC_CLOSE | prompt 5.5; UX1 (`ext-UX1-record.md`) |
| F83 | sndPlaySound (`platform/w16/user_misc.c`, `host_win32.c` host_play_sound; Q17) | The original's MMSYSTEM played through the machine's sound driver | PlaySoundW with SND_FILENAME and SND_NODEFAULT on the data directory's file, asynchronous when the game asks; a missing file is silent (no system default sound). "LOSE2.wav " (1150:0221) resolves to LOSE2.WAV (Q17). Mixing, latency and the driver's resampling are the host's (**choice**) | prompt 5.5; Q17 |
| F84 | WinHelp (1008:0373 HELP_QUIT, 1048:0539 HELP_CONTENTS; `user_misc.c`) | Windows 11 has no viewer for Windows 3.1 .hlp files | The call is recorded in the host log and returns TRUE, as when WINHELP.EXE took the command. HELP_CONTENTS (Help > Game Help) then shows a message box (OK, information icon, owned by the calling window) saying that help is not available because Windows 10 and 11 cannot show Windows 3.1 help files; HELP_QUIT at exit is silent; no other program is started (**choice**, prompt 5.5; the box was added in phase 8, audit A10; p4-menus closes it with Enter) | prompt 5.5 |
| F85 | Enter at a MenuZ dialog whose focus is not on its OK button (the title menu, dialog 139; MenuZ 1220:0AA0 loop, MenuZSubclassWndProc 1220:1289) | Which window had the focus there under Windows 3.1, and what its IsDialogMessage did with Enter, is not known | Keys go to the focus window (review P5MT-1). There, IsDialogMessage turns an Enter press into WM_COMMAND IDOK (the template's OK, id 101, is not a default push button), which MenuZ ignores; MenuZ acts on Enter when it arrives as a character: through the game's key queue (another game pump's ProcessKeyboardMessage, 1098:0339) or after a letter. Headless experiment: of four Enter presses at the title menu, the fourth started the game. **Closed 2026-10-10** by the user's observation of the original (`STATUS.md` user directions: the first Enter starts a new game, a second skips the Birth scenes once world creation is done). Cause: MenuZ's title mode (1220:1655) keeps WM_ACTIVATE from DefDlgProc, so the focus CreateDialog chose (the OK button) was not restored; activation now gives a dialog its saved control (**inferred**: Windows 3.1 set that focus during CreateDialog, before the dialog was shown). Headless after the fix: the first Enter press is WM_COMMAND 101 and the title menu closes at 22.0 s; the second skips the Birth scenes | review P5MT-1; `docs/verify/phase5-message-tables.md` |
| F86 | Which of the game's polls receives a key pressed in civ64 (`platform/w16/queue.c` w16_PeekMessage, `host_sync.c`; the MenuZ loop 1220:0AA0: IsKeyPending 11E8:02FB, whose ProcessKeyboardMessage 1098:02D1 peeks only key messages, then the loop's own PeekMessage 1220:0AC0 and IsDialogMessage 1220:0ADA) | Under Windows 3.1 input waited in the system queue until a GetMessage or PeekMessage took it; which of a loop's two polls took a key depended on where the program was when the key arrived, which is not known for the original's machines | Before 2026-10-10 civ64 moved host input into the emulated queue at the start of every PeekMessage: a key pressed at a MenuZ menu reached the key pump (the highlight moves, a letter selects its option) or IsDialogMessage (Down moved the focus off OK to the next control, letters did nothing) about equally often (agent-observed in civ64 windows). The original under OTVDM on Windows 11 (oracle run port-title, `build/oracle/port-title`, 2026-10-10): the focus stayed on OK, two Down presses moved the highlight to Play on EARTH, 'l' selected Load a Saved Game. Now a PeekMessage without a message range takes host input only when it ends finding nothing; one restricted to a range takes it when it starts; so in a loop that alternates the two, every key reaches the ranged pump first (**choice**, matching the oracle). Other sync points are unchanged; headless runs have no host input. Win32-headless: `test_host_win32` (F86 checks). civ64 after the change: Down, Down, 'l', Enter at the title menu about two to four seconds after it appears opened Load a Saved Game in two runs out of two. **Exception (P47b, 2026-10-15; `p47b-record.md`; F80):** an unranged PeekMessage that returns an idle repeat of the same undispatched WM_PAINT (no validation, no time charged since the previous retrieval) takes host input as a FALSE one does at its end; input taken there is returned by the next call, ahead of the WM_PAINT. MenuZ's loop is not affected (its key pump charges time between) (**choice**; Win32-headless: `test_host_win32`, P47b checks) | STATUS user directions (title-menu Enter); oracle port-title; `p47b-record.md` |
| F87 | Enter on a Civilopedia entry's first page (RunPediaModalLoop 1068:2728, the WM_CHAR 13 test on `g_msg` at 1068:27AB) | The loop tests the global `g_msg` for Enter even when PeekMessage found nothing (findings 6.5, **inferred** timing-dependent): page 2 closes at once unless some message (the Enter key up, a timer) is taken before that test. How long page 2 took to draw on the original's machines, and so how often this happened, is not known | Reproduced as the listing does it. civ64 draws page 2 in microseconds of real time, so a page-1 Enter press almost always closes page 2 too and returns to the list (agent-observed: frames 1005..1007 of run c7-menus within 17 ms); clicking OK with the mouse leaves a mouse message in `g_msg` and page 2 stays. The original under OTVDM on Windows 11 (oracle port-pedia, 2026-10-10): the page-1 Enter showed page 2, which stayed (a posted key up 50 ms later was pending). Not changed (**choice**: the port does not slow drawing; prompt section 4 item 9, CPU-speed-dependent behaviour) | findings (RunPediaModalLoop 1068:27AB); oracle port-pedia |

| F110 | the game screen follows the host window while the game runs (`platform/w16/user_wnd.c` `w16_screen_follow`, `follow_window`; `queue.c` w16_PeekMessage; host.h host_game_screen_follow; `present/present.c` present_game_screen_follow; DESIGN.md 12.4) | as F105 | Supersedes F105's once-per-run size (the user, 2026-10-11: the virtual game desktop "shall react to those actions by enlarging or reducing its resolution on the fly"). From the game's map window on, the game screen takes the client's size again whenever it changes (window sized, maximized, restored, full screen), at the scale of the game's start, or at the largest smaller one at which 640x480 still fits the client (a larger client never gives a larger scale). It happens at the game's own PeekMessage calls without a message range, outside the window manager's menu, scroll-bar and move/size loops: at an application's message loop, as Windows 3.1 delivered the Task List's Tile and Cascade. Then the visible top-level windows follow, through WM_SIZE and WM_MOVE as for the player's own moves: a maximized window is maximized again; an icon on the bottom row stays on it; a normal window's right or bottom edge on the old screen's edge stays on the edge (the map window by its size, any other by its position, as F105's layout rule); a window inside the old screen is kept inside, moved and, with a sizing border, sized; one wholly outside is moved inside (F96). What the screen gains is repainted. **Same seed:** the map window's size changes with the window during the game, so the random sequence of the map dissolve (F105) follows the player's window, as with the player's own sizing of the map window. The harness answers with its fixed `--game-screen` size or its fixed view, so it never follows and G-1 is unchanged. Tested: `test_user` `test_screen_follow`, `test_present` `game_screen_follow`, agent UI check (a loaded game in the 1920x1440 window: 640x480; maximized 853x509; restored 640x480; a 2400x1300 client 1200x650 at 2x) (**choice**, port adaptation, the user's requirement). **Hazard closed (P47b, 2026-10-15; `p47b-record.md`; F80):** a host resize while a dialog loop that does not dispatch is open (RunListBoxDialog 1098:1BEE: Find City, the Civilopedia's list) no longer hangs the game: the loop's repeats of the following window's WM_PAINT are idle polls that take host input, and the windows that follow are painted after the dialog closes (`p4tests.py` `listbar` `p49-findcity-resize`; user's check pending, `manual-checklist-ext.md` P47-2) | the user's requirement 2026-10-11; `p47b-record.md` |
| F111 | the host window's placement between runs (`platform/win32/host_win32.c` hw_set_placement, hw_placement, the settings hook on WM_EXITSIZEMOVE, maximize or restore and host_exit; `win64/civ64_main.c` `save_settings`, `remembered_placement`) | not part of the original | civ64 remembers the window's windowed placement in civ64.cfg (`window=l,t,r,b`, GetWindowPlacement's normal rectangle, and `maximized=0|1`) when the player ends a move or a size, maximizes or restores the window, and when the game ends; the next start takes it unless `--window` or `--scale` gives the first size, and only when the rectangle meets a monitor. With nothing remembered the window opens at the largest whole multiple of 640x480 that fits (4:3, the user's request of 2026-10-11). Full screen keeps its own setting and returns to that placement (the user, 2026-10-11: "host application shall remember its window size and position between the runs"). Tested: `test_host_win32` `test_placement`; agent check: taken exactly, maximized, a rectangle on no monitor ignored. **Revised in LP (2026-10-11 22:15 and 22:20; `hw_place_decide`, `hw_create_window_ex`, DESIGN.md 12.2):** (1) the first start is centred horizontally and vertically in the **primary monitor's work area** (`rcWork`, so a top or left taskbar is not covered; never above or left of it), also when `--window` or `--scale k` gave the size ("The host application shall start both vertically and horizontally centered on the screen on initial startup"); `fit_scale`'s default size now follows the primary monitor too (it used the monitor under the cursor); (2) the remembered rectangle is in **screen coordinates** (`hw_placement` converts `WINDOWPLACEMENT`'s workspace coordinates by the primary monitor's work-area offset; the start uses `SetWindowPos`, no longer `SetWindowPlacement`; a `window` value written by an earlier version under a top or left taskbar is read as screen coordinates once, shifted by the taskbar's size, then rewritten); (3) the display check is the user's rule, "\"entirely off-screen\" is the rule": a rectangle that meets no monitor (no overlap of positive area with any monitor rectangle, no monitor known included) goes to the work area's top-left corner ("it should re align itself top left corner of the primary screen on startup"), keeping the remembered size when it fits the work area, else the default size clamped to it; a window only partly off-screen, or straddling two monitors, keeps its position and size (the coordinator's first draft, "any part outside", was replaced by the rule at 22:20); the maximized state is kept, and a rectangle that went to the corner maximizes on the primary monitor, while a rectangle on an on-screen secondary monitor, maximized, maximizes on that monitor, as before. These are user requests and recorded choices ("top left" = the work area's top-left; "the screen" = the primary work area), not original behaviour. Tested in LP: 25 `hw_place_decide` checks (taskbars at the bottom, top and left; remembered inside; straddling; partly off-screen kept; entirely off-screen, edge-touching only, bigger than the work area, no monitor; a negative-coordinate monitor to the left of the primary) and real hidden-window cases (first start centred, also with `--window`; remembered placement taken; entirely off-screen at the work area's top-left; partly off-screen kept). **Not exercised:** a non-zero workspace offset (a top or left taskbar; offset 0 on the test machine, so that case rests on the synthetic tests and the documentation), a monitor of a different DPI, a monitor removed or added between runs, full screen combined with the new placement beyond the existing test, `WM_DPICHANGED` on a remembered window (**choice**, the user's request). **Extension UX4** (2026-10-16, the user's LP-8 result): a remembered rectangle is kept only while Windows gives it as it is (not larger than the largest window, `WM_GETMINMAXINFO`) and a usable part of its title bar strip (at least half its height, twice its height wide) lies in a monitor's work area; else the primary work area's top-left corner (LP's size rule); the rectangle Windows gave is checked again after the move; the value written back is the window's real rectangle (`hw_place_decide` with `HwPlaceFit`, `hw_place_usable`, `hw_place_corner`, `HW_PLACE_MOVED`; DESIGN.md 12.2). The key's format: `window=left,top,right,bottom`, the outer rectangle in screen pixels, the primary screen's top-left 0,0, right and bottom exclusive. Status: landed 2026-10-16 (the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 15:27:18 UTC, `build/ux4-status.txt`; `w32_test_host_win32` `test_place_decide`, with two LP expectations changed: a rectangle larger than the largest window and one pixel of title bar on a monitor now go to the corner, and `test_placement`); the user's checks LP-8, UX4-1 and UX4-2 passed 2026-10-16 about 15:38..15:39 UTC (build `build/user-builds/ux4/`) | the user's request 2026-10-11; LP (`ext-LP-record.md`); UX4 (`ext-UX4-record.md`) |

## Extension D1: recorded design choices (implemented in later phases)

Entries F113 onwards record the deviations and recorded choices of the extension's D1 design
(DESIGN.md 12.5..12.11; drafts `verify/ext-d1-draft-A.md`..`-E.md`, Revision 3; resolutions in
`verify/ext-d1-decisions.md`). Each carries the phase that implements it; the status of an entry
already implemented names its gate record (F119, F120 and F124: D2; F114, F116, F117, F118 and the tick of
F128: D3, `ext-D3-acceptance.md`; F129, F130 and F132: M, `ext-M-acceptance.md`, which also extends F114;
F125's clock and omitted title waits: D4b, `ext-D4b1-record.md` and `ext-D4b2b2-record.md`; D3 also moved F100's and F101's code to `present/arrive.c`, above). F188 (unit UX4b's recorded deviation, 2026-10-16) stands next to F114.
Items the user is to check are listed in `manual-checklist-ext.md`.

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F113 | invariant P0 (extension 4); `present/present.c` `present_game_screen(_follow)`; harness `--game-screen` | Since phase G the virtual screen follows the host client during play (F105, F110), so the host size and the scaling policy are core inputs under `--game-screen view` | P0 is scoped: the graphics profile, the switch history, the map zoom, full screen and the filter never change checkpoints, the virtual screen or the base trace in any mode; the host size and the policy do not with a fixed game screen (the harness default or `--game-screen WxH`). `present_game_screen(_follow)` never reads the profile, the shown frame or the zoom (**choice**, decision K1; DESIGN.md 12.11). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D3 (7.3 runner) and checked at every later gate | K1; A 1, 13.1 |
| F114 | the Ctrl and Shift keys and mouse modifier flags (`present/arrive.c` `pi_arrive_key`, `pi_arrive_mouse`; next to F101) | Windows 3.1 posted WM_KEYDOWN VK_CONTROL/VK_SHIFT when the key went down and set MK_CONTROL/MK_SHIFT in mouse messages while it was held; the profile chord Ctrl+Shift+Backspace would otherwise reach the game, and PumpMessagesCheckSkipInput 11E0:095F skips movies on any key | F101's Alt rule extends to Ctrl and Shift: a press is held back until the next key event; the chord (and from M the zoom chords) never reaches the game; another key or a mouse press delivers the held presses first, in order; a lone Ctrl or Shift is delivered as press and release together at its release, so it still skips the intro, at the release; while a press is held back, MK_CONTROL/MK_SHIFT of that key are cleared in mouse moves and releases. The game sees the modifier late; GetKeyState would read it up early (the core does not call it) (**choice**, decisions K3 and RV1-15; user check). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D3 (D3b2, 2026-10-11, `ext-D3-acceptance.md`; `test_input`, V7, `ext_adapt.py`). As built: a held key released alone is delivered after the presses held before it (Ctrl held, Shift held, Shift released: Ctrl, Shift, then Shift's release); Alt+Enter serves only the held Alt, so a Shift or Ctrl held before it is delivered at its release or before the next key, as in S2; a Backspace already delivered down makes no chord (its release must reach the game); a press that is not delivered (outside the image, F100) leaves the held presses held until the next key or delivered press. From M (Ma, 2026-10-11, `ext-M-acceptance.md`) the rule covers the zoom chords Ctrl+Shift+Plus, Minus and 0 (main row and keypad): `ext_adapt.py`'s zoomkeys run (eight chords, with repeats) shows their presses, repeats and releases and the held modifiers' releases not reaching the game, while the plain `=`, `-`, `0` and keypad keys do. With Num Lock on, Shift turns keypad 0 into `VK_INSERT` on usual layouts, so the keypad reset may not arrive (the main-row 0 does). Under `CIV_OBSERVER 0` these keys are not chords and reach the game. **Extension UX4** (2026-10-16, the user's M-3 result): the keypad's 0 makes Ctrl+Shift+0 as `VK_INSERT` with scan 0x52 (not extended; Num Lock off, or on with Windows' Shift release, which the arrival stage holds back and drops for this chord only); the gray Insert key stays the game's. Windows' own Shift release is marked by `PiKey.fake` (lParam bit 25, or a Shift release followed in the window's queue by a keypad key at the same message time); every other next event takes the held release first, so every other input gives the events of before UX4. The main row's Ctrl+Shift+0 does not reach civ64's window on the user's PC (a keyboard tool takes it; not reachable without a global hook): the user chose Ctrl+Shift+Home as an extra reset chord (2026-10-16 about 15:05 UTC; unit UX4b). Status: landed 2026-10-16 (the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 15:27:18 UTC, `build/ux4-status.txt`; `test_input` 435, `w32_test_host_win32` `test_ux4_keys`); the user's check M-3 passed 2026-10-16 about 15:39 UTC (build `build/user-builds/ux4/`). From UX4b also Ctrl+Shift+Home (`VK_HOME`, gray and keypad with Num Lock off): **extension UX4b** (2026-10-16, the user's choice of about 15:05 UTC) takes `VK_HOME` whatever its scan code (the gray Home 0x147, the keypad's 7 with Num Lock off 0x47) as one more reset chord, exactly as Ctrl+Shift+0 (`zoom_key`'s `case PI_VK_HOME`: the same command `PI_CMD_ZOOM_RESET` and hold rule; no chord with Alt down, with Home already delivered down, or under `CIV_OBSERVER 0`); the keypad's 7 with Num Lock on is the digit 7 and no chord (Windows' Shift release is taken first, so Shift is up). The window menu's item reads "&Normal map zoom\tCtrl+Shift+0, Ctrl+Shift+Home". Its effect on the Windows controls that read Home is the recorded deviation F188. Status: designed in D1; implemented in D3 (D3b2, 2026-10-11, `ext-D3-acceptance.md`). As built, the item runs `civ64_locate_dos_choose`, the choose-and-validate loop of the start-up question without its "not found" question: a file dialog and messages owned by the game window (the game window waits disabled under the dialog); a choice is validated against the pinned DOS version, an invalid one is refused with its message and asked again, cancel records nothing; a valid one sets `dos_data`, removes `dos_ask=never`, saves `civ64.cfg` and says "The DOS files are recorded. The DOS graphics become available the next time civ64 starts."; the DOS store, the running game and its profiles are untouched. The item is grayed in a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build, which has no DOS profile for the location to serve. Since HM (landed 2026-10-17, the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 19:22:16 UTC, `build/hm-status.txt`; the user's rule of about 10:08 UTC; `ext-HM-record.md` Decision 4) the item is the Graphics submenu's last, written "Locate DOS files…" (U+2026); it reads "DOS files present", disabled, while civ64's hook `hw_set_dos_present_hook` answers 1: the run's DOS graphics are available (`pdos_assets_status() == PDOS_OK`, the files found at start-up) or a Locate succeeded in this run; files removed from the disk while civ64 runs do not change it; its command then does nothing; a `CIV_OBSERVER 0` build keeps "Locate DOS files…" grayed whatever the hook says (F258) | RV1-6; A 8.2, 10.2; E.3.6; HM (`ext-HM-record.md`) |

| F119 | a DOS folder inside the game's data directory (`win64/civ64_locate.c` `civ64_dos_dir_ok`, called by `win64/civ64_main.c` after `civ64_open_data`; E.3.3) | Extension 5.9 says the DOS directory "must not be the data directory or lie inside it" | Only a DOS folder equal to the data directory is refused (message box, exit 2); a folder inside it (for example a `CivDos` subfolder of the program folder used as data directory) is accepted, because the core cannot reach it: the virtual DOS resolves only `\CIV\NAME` (depth 2) and `host_dir_list` lists the top level only. Both final paths (`GetFinalPathNameByHandleW`: junctions, links and substituted drives resolved) are compared ignoring case; when either cannot be resolved the folder is accepted (nothing to compare); a ZIP source, held in memory, is not compared. Tested: `w32_test_dos_host` (equal, with a trailing separator and in another case refused; inside accepted) (**choice**, decision E.18-1). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D2 (ext-D2-acceptance.md; the function lies in `civ64_locate.c`, not in `civ64_start.c` as designed) | E.18-1; E E.3.3, E.13.3 |
| F120 | reading the DOS files (`host_dos_read`, both back ends: `platform/win32/host_win32.c`, `tests/headless/host_headless.c`; E.3.5) | Rule 2.3: the DOS files are never written | Every access is a read-only open (Win32: `CreateFileW` with `GENERIC_READ`, `FILE_SHARE_READ`, `OPEN_EXISTING`; headless: `_open` with `_O_RDONLY`); a ZIP source is read once, read-only, by phase R or the harness and then used from memory; nothing writes the files. A read can still update a file's last-access time where the volume keeps it (an NTFS policy); a read-only handle cannot prevent that (`SetFileTime(-1)` needs `FILE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES`). The session hash check compares attributes and `LastWriteTimeUtc` only; `test_dos` compares the size, SHA-256 and modification time of all 176 files of `build/dosdata` before and after its run, and `w32_test_dos_host` checks `CIV.EXE`'s size, last-write time and attributes after its reads (**inferred**, host fact; Open E5). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D2 (ext-D2-acceptance.md) | E5; E E.3.5 |
| F121 | the 6-bit DAC values of every DOS palette (`present/dos/pdos_pal.c` `pdos_v8`; `gen/dos/png.py vga6_to_8`) | The DAC holds 6-bit values with linear output, full scale 63 (PC fact); the DOS listing does not scale them, so no DOS rule exists for 8-bit colour | One rule for every conversion: round to nearest, `v8 = (v * 255 + 31) / 63` (no ties, since 63 is odd); the D0 viewers' floor rendering is relabelled as a viewer choice and changed to this rule at D2. Floor and bit replication differ in 30 and 10 of the 64 values (**inferred**, platform fact; rounding a **choice**, B1; OPEN P23). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D2 (viewers) and D3 (`pdos_pal`) | P23; B 5.1 |
| F122 | core text drawn with DOS fonts (`present/dos/pdos_charmap.c`; B 10.4) | Every DOS font ends at code 7Fh or lower and G#21 turns bytes of 80h and above into colour changes; the DOS program has no substitution; core text (names typed by the user) is Windows-1252 | A fixed fold of core text to DOS-drawable bytes before measuring, wrapping and truncating: accented letters to their base letter, ligatures and special letters to two letters (Æ → AE, ß → ss), quotes, dashes, ellipsis, no-break space and a few symbols to ASCII forms, everything else 80h..FFh and control bytes to `?`, tab to space. DOS static text is drawn raw; name entry passes the core's text unchanged (**choice**, B13; OPEN P13). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D4 | P13; B 10.4 |

The low-memory look is not modelled: a DOS machine with little free memory would cut rows 0..5 with one variant too (Open E7). `registry.py check` confirms the test of DGROUP:1A3E at O5:152B in the listing; `test_dos` checks TERRAIN 16t + m equal to 16t for t 6..9 and distinct for t 0..5, and every shape against the independent model `gen/dos/shapes.py` (**choice**, E.9.1, RV1-16). | F125 | DOS timing in the DOS profile (`present/dos/pdos_clock.c`, `pdos_tl.c`; C.4.1, C.7.4) | The DOS program measures its own display and runs on real hardware, so its timing varies with the machine | An ideal DOS machine: a VGA frame of 17,024 PIT clocks (about 70.086 Hz), NSOUND's sound tick on six of seven frames with the divider reset at every tune start, full-palette fade batches (`E` = 256), DOS code, retrace waits, palette loads, the key flush and the dissolve's BIOS wait and calibration taking no time, the first fade of a session one frame longer (rules M1..M12). After a title choice the DOS confirmation wait, phrase-boundary wait and tune stop are omitted: the box stays until the core's next scene (**choice**, decision C.20-8; P1, P2, P7, P29). Status: designed in D1; implemented in D4. The clock was built in D4b1 (`pdos_clock`, `pdos_tl`; `ext-D4b1-record.md`) as a model, label **DOS listing (model)**: the 17,024 is TIMING 2.2's inferred VGA value; DOS code, retrace waits, loads and the key flush take no time (M2, M3, M11); fades write the whole palette each frame (P7) and the session's first fade takes one more frame (M4); the dissolve copies floor(8,800 / c) pixels a frame (M10; c = 0 and a dissolve of page 0 are instant); the sound-tick divider restarts at each tune start (P29). A timeline's state at a time depends only on the inputs up to it (F148). The omitted waits after a title choice were built with the title box in D4b2b2 (F149). No original timing comparison was made (the DOS oracle is optional and was not run; C-2 open) | C.20-8; C C.4.1, C.7.4 |

## Extension D2: DOS assets

Entries F139 onwards record the choices of extension phase D2, the DOS asset store `present/dos/`
(DESIGN.md 12.6; gate record `ext-D2-acceptance.md`, 2026-10-11), that the D1 entries above do not
cover. D2 also implemented F119, F120 and F124. Every entry below is implemented in D2 and tested
headless (`test_dos`, `w32_test_dos_host`; Checked and Release); none changes the core's run.

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F139 | the sprite arena of the start-up cut (`present/dos/pdos_shapes.c`; O5:1455, arena opened by `G#18` at O5:146F; E.9.1) | `G#18` takes the largest free DOS block for the arena and `G#2` GrabSprite returns 0 when the arena is full (DRIVER G#2, G#18), so on a machine with little memory later grabs would fail | Every grab succeeds: the store holds all 525 cut shapes (114,709 bytes; 586 of the 644 entries filled, the other 58 never cut by DOS). The arena-full case is not modelled (**not modelled**; inferred: the case on any machine that had the memory for the 256-colour mode; Open E8) | E8; E E.9.1 |
| F140 | how a shape is stored (`present/dos/pdos_shapes.h` `PdosShape`; E.9.1) | `G#2` stores each row of a shape without its leading and trailing zeros, and `G#32` / `G#6` draw the stored pixels with inner zeros transparent (DRIVER 3.3; SPRITES 1) | Each shape is a full w x h array of palette indices in which 0 is transparent; it draws the same pixels as the trimmed DOS rows. The DOS sprite memory layout is not reproduced (**choice**, representation only) | E E.9.1 |
| F141 | stale state of the DOS picture reader (`present/dos/pdos_pic.c` `pdos_pic_decode`: chunk walk R:0000:109E..1135, LZW R:0000:1318; E.6.2 "Refusals") | The DOS reader reads words from a 512-byte buffer that a refill past the end of the file leaves stale, keeps the previous code [68F8] across pictures (never reset; an entry made right after a start or a reset takes it as prefix), and copies every chunk over the dictionary area DGROUP:BA06..D205 (6,144 bytes) and beyond (FORMATS 1.3) | Input whose DOS result would depend on that state, or overrun that area, is refused instead of modelled (`PDOS_E_FORMAT` naming the file; the store stays closed): a header or chunk word past the end of the file; a second `M0` chunk (DOS would load the union of both ranges into the DAC); an `M0` shorter than its first..last range (stale buffer bytes), or longer than 770 bytes (a choice: the size of the kept array and of every pinned `M0`; DOS copies any length); a first LZW code above FFh after a start or a reset; an expansion through entry 100h made from the previous dictionary's code; a code using a bit of the missing byte of an odd-sized file's half last word; a chunk over 6,144 bytes. So a picture decodes the same in any order. None of the pinned pictures and palettes is refused (**choice**, E.6.2) | E E.6.2, E.11 |
| F142 | the DOS section lookup (`present/dos/pdos_text.c` `pdos_section_lookup`, `pdos_sections_parse`; R:1F4D:01AD, collection loop R:1F4D:032D..0388; E.8.2) | The DOS lookup probes its index without a bound (a full index would loop for ever, FORMATS 3.4), writes what it finds into the index (its cache write), and its collection loop does not stop at the end of the file (FORMATS 3.6) | The lookup runs read-only (without the cache write) once per section name at activation and its result is kept. After 256 index probes it falls back to the scan from 0200h. A name whose group of `*` records reaches the end of the file shows nothing (not found, -1), where DOS would not return; the section files' end markers are such names. A text that reaches the end of the file is refused (`PDOS_E_FORMAT`), as is a section file shorter than 526 bytes (the probed index words 0..262) or whose first record at 0200h is empty (the DOS scan would not advance) (**choice**, E.8.2; ext-D2-acceptance.md deviation E.8.2) | E E.8.2; FORMATS 3.4, 3.6 |
| F143 | `CIV_UNP.EXE` in place of `CIV.EXE` (`present/dos/pdos_assets.c` `pdos_assets_open`; `platform/win32/host_win32.c` `hw_set_dos_source`, `tests/headless/host_headless.c` `hh_set_dos_zip`; E.5.3) | Extension 5.9 accepts the unpacked `CIV_UNP.EXE` (the user's copy, EXECUTABLE.md) instead of `CIV.EXE`; phase R's validity rule asks for `CIV.EXE` (decision E.18-2) | When `CIV.EXE` is missing, the store reads the pinned `CIV_UNP.EXE` (optional in `tests/dos-files.txt`), takes its load module as the root, checked against the same derived hashes of the root and its relocations, and its overlays from its tail: the content equals `CIV.EXE`'s, overlay bases aside. A `CIV.EXE` that is present but different is still refused; a different `CIV_UNP.EXE` is refused by name; with both missing the failure names `CIV.EXE`. A ZIP without `civ.exe` uses the folder of its one `CIV_UNP.EXE` (both back ends). civ64's own lookup (phase R, DESIGN.md 12.3) still requires the pinned `CIV.EXE`, so the alternative is reached in headless runs and tests (**choice**, E.5.3, E.18-2) | E E.5.3, E.18-2 |

So a shortcut counts only when it arrives before frame 3,912's instant; one at that instant only ends the hold. below is compiled and tested headless and Win32-headless in Checked (the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN,

user's correction of UX1-1; record `ext-UX1-record.md` "UX1b") revises F159; it landed 2026-10-16 with D5a..D5c in
one build slot, compiled and tested headless and Win32-headless in Checked (the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN,
`build/d5l-status.txt`, 2026-10-16 01:20:28 UTC); the user's check UX1-1 is pending again (build
`build/user-builds/d5c/`).

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F159 | the DOS pointer's displayed size (`present/scale.c` `present_pointer_factors_of`, `present_cursor_factors_of`; `present/present.c` `present_pointer_factors`; `present/dos/pdos_cursor.c`; `platform/win32/host_win32.c` `cursor_factors`) | Before UX1 the DOS pointer (ICON 7, the torch; ICON 2, the GO arrow) was drawn at the visible layout's cursor factors (DESIGN.md 12.2 "Cursor"; a 1x image on the N frame, a 2x image on the A frame); a 320x200 frame's factor is about twice the 640x480 frame's at the same host size, so the torch was about twice as large, relative to the window, as the Windows game cursor. The user (2026-10-15 about 14:03 UTC): "make dos cursor size approx. windows counterpart with integer scaled down. (without loosing its fidelity)". The user's correction of UX1's size (2026-10-15 about 23:15 UTC, UX1-1): "the DOS torch pointer is too small and shall be on the size between the previous and the current one" | **Since UX1b** (the user's correction; `present_pointer_factors_of(V, W)` is the only change, `present.c` and `host_win32.c` unchanged): per axis k = clamp(1, 16, ceil((k_before + k_UX1) / 2)) = clamp(1, 16, (k_before + k_UX1 + 1) div 2), the arithmetic mean of the two factors with a half rounded up, toward the larger factor because UX1's pointer was found too small (the orchestrator's reading of "between"). k_UX1 is UX1's factor (below). k_before is the factor before UX1: on a 320x200 frame (the N frame: the DOS title box, the intro, Birth) that frame's own cursor factors, `present_cursor_factors_of(V)` (the old 1x image at the visible layout's factors; at 4:3 its ky includes the frame's taller pixels, so the old ky and UX1's aspect-corrected ky are averaged alike); on any other frame (the A frame: the Windows layout, 640x480 or the game screen of F105/F110) twice the Windows cursor's factors, 2 · `present_cursor_factors_of(W)` (the old 2x image at the A frame's factors). The frame kind is V's size: exactly 320x200 is the N frame (a **choice**; no new inputs). UX1's factor is never above the factor before UX1, so the pointer is never smaller than UX1's and never larger than before UX1; where the two differ by 1 no whole factor lies between and the mean rounded up gives the size from before UX1 (2x2: at 640x480 everywhere; on the A frames under integer up to 1366x768); a size strictly between appears where they differ by 2 or more. Under civ64's defaults (integer, nearest, 4:3; F99, X3) the pointer is, on the 320x200 frames, 2x2 up to 1366x768, 3x4 at 1920x1080, 5x5 at 2560x1440 and 6x8 at 3840x2160, and on the A frames 2x2 up to 1366x768, 3x3, 5x5 and 6x6; the table of every 7.1 size, combination and frame kind (before UX1 / UX1 / UX1b) is the record's UX1b section. Still one DOS pointer pixel a block of whole host pixels, never filtered, the hot spot multiplied by the same factors (`pixel_cursor`); the game's own cursor and the moments of recomputation as UX1's. **UX1's rule** (2026-10-15, kept as the history): one DOS pointer pixel is drawn as a block of whole host pixels of the Windows game cursor's pixel size at the same host size, never filtered. `pdos_cursor` gives the shape's own pixels on both frames (one argb pixel per DOS pixel, hot spot `PDOS_POINTER_HOT_X/Y`, (0, 0); the A frame's 2x is gone); the back end makes the pointer at `present_pointer_factors_of(V, W)`, V the visible frame's layout, W the Windows frame's layout at its current size (640x480 or the game screen of F105/F110) in the same client with the same policy, filter and aspect: kx = clamp(1, 16, (2·W.sw + W.w) div (2·W.w)), the Windows cursor's horizontal factor; ky = clamp(1, 16, nearest(kyW · a)), kyW = (2·W.sh + W.h) div (2·W.h), a = (V.sh · V.w) / (V.h · V.sw) the visible image's pixel aspect, a half toward kyW. a is 1 except for a 320x200 frame at 4:3 (1.17..1.25), so on square pixels the pointer has exactly the Windows cursor's factors and at 4:3 it is as tall as the frame's own pixels allow. Under civ64's defaults (integer, nearest, 4:3; F99, X3) the pointer is 1x1 from 640x480 to 1366x768, 2x2 at 1920x1080, 3x3 at 2560x1440 and 4x5 at 3840x2160 (before UX1: 2x2 at 640x480 and 800x600, 3x3 from 1024x768 to 1366x768, then 4x5, 6x7 and 8x10); the table of every 7.1 size and combination is the record's 1. The hot spot is multiplied by the same factors; the game's own cursor (the Windows profile, the DOS profile's scroll cursors 136..143) keeps the visible layout's cursor factors; the factors are computed again on WM_SIZE (resize, maximize, full screen, DPI change), an option change, a frame-size change (N and A) and a profile switch. The DOS program's pointer relative to its 320x200 screen is larger (one DOS pixel per screen pixel). P0 unaffected: presentation and host cursor only, mouse mapping unchanged. A new drop-down opens at row 0, except the first level opened by a press on the bar while the button is down, which opens with no row (the DOS box's mouse code runs before its first highlight and the press lies outside the box; `W16qMenu.mouse`, a read-only field of `w16q_menu_state`, tells which); the Options box opens at row 0 or, opened again after a toggle, at the toggled row ([2F9A]; F256). | F255 | the mouse in the DOS menus (ruling M2; `present/dos/pdos_reskin.c` `pdos_reskin_menu_mouse`, replacing `pdos_reskin_menu_point`, and `pdos_menubox_row`; I-menubar/press and I-menubar/title in `present/dos/pdos_adapt.c`; DOS R:1D05:016A..01D9, R:0403:0676; D6fS-b's item F-new-5) | DOS opens a menu at a press on the bar and its box loop chooses at the release, so a quick click on a title ends the menu at once (the release lies outside the box: -1) and DOS's menus are used by dragging; DOS's row = (my - y - 4) / h - [B1EC] by idiv truncates toward zero, so the bar's y 5..7 over a headerless box at y 8 are its row 0; DOS takes the menu once, at the press, so with the button held over another title its box sees the pointer outside (-1) | The w16 menu loop's click model is kept (ruling M2): a click on a title opens its menu and leaves it open, a click on a row chooses it; dragging works as in DOS. On the deepest drop-down a press and a held move put the DOS highlight on the row under the point (a disabled row keeps the previous one, the header and the frame none) and go to the w16 loop at the Windows popup's top-left (on no item); the release on an enabled row with a command goes to the centre of its Windows item (an Options row also asks for the reopening, F256), on a row without a command to a corner off the menus (the loop ends), on a disabled row or a grayed item to the popup's top-left (nothing); a press or release on a lower drop-down or on a hidden popup outside every drop-down (F229) goes to a corner off the menus, a move there does nothing. The bar's y 5..7 stay the bar's (the zone's title); a held move over the bar goes to the zone's Windows title, so the loop switches menus as Windows does (a hover without the button switches nothing) (**choice**, ruling M2; recorded deviations). ## Extension SI: one civ64 at a time

Entry F189 records extension unit SI (the user's direction of 2026-10-16 about 19:44 UTC, STATUS.md "User
directions"; DESIGN.md 12.2 "Single instance"; RUNNING.md "One civ64 at a time"; record `ext-SI-record.md`, session
E3, 2026-10-16). Host only: the core, the presentation, the game's files and the saves are unchanged. Landed
2026-10-16 (the copy-in at 22:42:18 UTC; the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 23:38:15 UTC, `build/si-status.txt`);
compiled and tested headless and Win32-headless in Checked (CheckedNoObs and Release only privately in the stage), and
in the stage's private check with real civ64.exe processes, each on a desktop of its own (never on the user's
screen); no DOS program was executed. User checks SI-1..SI-6 pending (`manual-checklist-ext.md`, user build
`build/user-builds/si/`).

| Id | Address | Reason | Port behaviour | Ref |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F189 | one civ64 at a time (`win64/civ64_start.c` extension SI: `civ64_single_instance`, `civ64_si_game_texts`, `civ64_si_win32`, `civ64_si_publish_texts`; `win64/civ64_main.c`; WinMain 1008:0070..0087) | Civilization for Windows refuses a second game itself: WinMain 1008:0070, when `hPrevInstance` is not 0, calls MessageBox(NULL, DS:0056 "Sorry! Only one game at a time!", DS:0076 "CIVILIZATION Error", MB_ICONHAND) and returns 0 (1008:0077 `push 0056h`, 1008:007B `push 0076h`; **binary proof**); under Windows 3.1 a second start showed that box and ended, without bringing the running game forward. In civ64 each process is its own emulated Windows 3.1: the emulated InitTask's `hPrevInstance` is 0 (`platform/w16/kernel_task.c`), so that check never fires, and before SI any number of civ64 processes ran side by side. The user (2026-10-16 about 19:44 UTC): "Host application shall only be single instance" | civ64 runs once per Windows logon session. The first civ64 owns the named mutex `Local\civ64.single-instance.{9C2D6E41-7A35-4F08-B1E6-2D84C0F3A957}` (taken without waiting; one abandoned by an ended holder counts as owned) to the end of its process and publishes, in the named shared block `...owner` (120 bytes), its process id and creation time and, once its civ.exe is loaded (right after `ne_load`), the game's refusal text and caption read from its loaded DGROUP (DS:0056, 31 characters; DS:0076, 18 characters; no text of the original in the port's sources), each part with its own "written" mark. A second start ends with code 0 right after its command line is read, before civ64.cfg is read, the game's files are looked for or a window is made; its command line is ignored, even a wrong one (no usage box). The same program file (the same path ignoring case, or the same file under another name) shows the original's box: the running civ64's published text and caption, the stop icon and OK (`MB_OK` with WinMain's `MB_ICONHAND`), through civ64's host box `hw_message_box`, owned by no window; after OK it brings the running civ64 forward (`ShowWindowAsync(SW_RESTORE)`, which returns a minimized civ64 to its previous state, maximized or full screen included, then `SetForegroundWindow` on its last active popup: its open box or dialog if it has one, else the frame) and ends with code 0, as the original ends after its box. Another program file shows civ64's own box in its place (one box, never two: caption "Sid Meier's Civilization 64bit", the information icon, naming the running program's folder and asking to close that civ64 before starting this copy), then the activation; when the running program's path cannot be read, the original's box. While the holder has not published itself or its texts yet, or has no visible window yet, the start looks again every 100 ms for up to 5 s (`CIV64_SI_WAIT_MS`, `CIV64_SI_STEP_MS`); a holder that has not loaded its civ.exe by then (at its start-up questions) gives no box, only the activation. A civ64 that ends, normally or not, frees the lock (Windows releases the mutex and the block): one that ends while the start waits or while its box is open lets this start run. No lock possible (CreateMutex fails): the start runs. The test option `--multi-instance` neither takes nor checks the lock (`tests/win32/drive_civ64.py` passes it). The core's own check stays as translated and still never fires (P0). **Deviation:** only the activation after the box (the running civ64 restored and brought to the front); **reason:** the user asked for a single instance, and the original's box alone leaves the running game hidden (behind other windows, or minimized). Known limits: a civ64 started as another Windows user in the same session, or a plain start while a civ64 runs elevated, may be refused access to the other's mutex and then runs as a second civ64; another user's session has its own `Local\` (each signed-in user can run one civ64) (**choice**, the user's direction of 2026-10-16 about 19:44 UTC, the orchestrator's ruling and its decision on the record's Decision 9: follow the original). Status: implemented in SI (landed 2026-10-16: copy-in 22:42:18 UTC, the three-step cycle ALL_GREEN 23:38:15 UTC, `build/si-status.txt`); tests `w32_test_single` (50 checks: the decision's cases, the switch, the text, the original's refusal from civ.exe, the processes); user checks SI-1..SI-6 pending (build `build/user-builds/si/`) | the user's direction 2026-10-16; `ext-SI-record.md` (Findings 1 and 6, Decisions 1..9) |
